## Response:
7/16/2026, 8:22:45 AM

# Seri 12/14 - Maintenance & Observability: VACUUM, ANALYZE, Bloat, Size Skew, Default Partition Monitoring, Catalog Query, Alerting, dan Runbook Production

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini fokus ke sisi operasional:

> **Bagaimana menjaga partitioned table tetap sehat setelah production berjalan?**

Partitioning bukan “sekali desain lalu selesai”. Setelah table dipartisi, kita harus terus memantau:

```text id="rmj9hg"
1. Apakah partition aktif terlalu besar?
2. Apakah future partition tersedia?
3. Apakah default partition mulai terisi?
4. Apakah autovacuum/analyze berjalan?
5. Apakah statistics stale?
6. Apakah index bloat/unused/terlalu besar?
7. Apakah query masih pruning dengan benar?
8. Apakah partition size skew muncul?
9. Apakah retention job berjalan?
10. Apakah DDL/index/attach/detach menimbulkan lock?
```

PostgreSQL menjelaskan bahwa routine vacuuming penting untuk beberapa hal: reclaim/update ruang akibat row update/delete, memperbarui planner statistics, memperbarui visibility map untuk index-only scan, dan mencegah transaction ID wraparound. Autovacuum dapat otomatis menjalankan `VACUUM` dan `ANALYZE`, tetapi table/partition besar tetap perlu observability yang jelas. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/routine-vacuuming.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 2. Mental Model Maintenance Partitioned Table

```text id="j3ip1k"
Partitioned Table Maintenance
│
├─ Per-parent monitoring
│  ├─ partition key
│  ├─ partition count
│  ├─ future horizon
│  ├─ default partition
│  └─ global query patterns
│
├─ Per-partition monitoring
│  ├─ table size
│  ├─ index size
│  ├─ estimated row count
│  ├─ live/dead tuples
│  ├─ vacuum/analyze timestamps
│  ├─ index usage
│  └─ bloat indication
│
├─ Planner health
│  ├─ stats freshness
│  ├─ EXPLAIN baseline
│  ├─ pruning evidence
│  └─ bad query detection
│
├─ Lifecycle health
│  ├─ future partition creation
│  ├─ retention/detach/drop
│  ├─ archive verification
│  └─ legal hold check
│
└─ Operational safety
   ├─ lock monitoring
   ├─ long transaction monitoring
   ├─ autovacuum progress
   ├─ index build progress
   └─ incident runbook
```

Key point:

```text id="kx6p1j"
Pada partitioned table, maintenance harus dipikirkan per partition.
Current partition, historical partition, default partition, dan future partition punya karakter berbeda.
```

---

# 3. VACUUM dan ANALYZE: Apa yang Harus Dipahami

## 3.1 VACUUM

`VACUUM` membersihkan dead tuples dari update/delete dan membantu mencegah transaction ID wraparound. Pada partitioned table, dead tuples biasanya terkonsentrasi di hot/current partitions atau table queue-like seperti outbox/inbox.

```sql id="wp0u49"
VACUUM app.audit_event_p2026_07;
```

Untuk detail:

```sql id="ws4qte"
VACUUM (VERBOSE) app.audit_event_p2026_07;
```

## 3.2 ANALYZE

`ANALYZE` mengumpulkan statistics yang dipakai planner untuk memilih plan. Setelah bulk load, attach partition, atau data distribution berubah signifikan, jalankan `ANALYZE`.

```sql id="o4ke44"
ANALYZE app.audit_event_p2026_07;
```

PostgreSQL documentation menyebut autovacuum daemon, jika aktif, otomatis menjalankan `ANALYZE` ketika isi table berubah cukup banyak. Namun untuk partition yang baru di-load/attach, manual `ANALYZE` sering diperlukan agar planner tidak membuat keputusan buruk. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/routine-vacuuming.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 3.3 VACUUM FULL

`VACUUM FULL` rewrite table dan mengambil lock lebih berat. Jangan jadikan default maintenance.

```sql id="lv8q3a"
VACUUM FULL app.audit_event_p2026_07;
```

Gunakan hanya setelah evaluasi matang, biasanya pada maintenance window, karena bisa memblokir operasi normal.

Practical rule:

```text id="tks292"
Untuk partitioned table:
- regular VACUUM/ANALYZE = routine
- VACUUM FULL = exceptional operation
- REINDEX CONCURRENTLY = sering lebih aman untuk index bloat
- detach/drop partition = lebih baik untuk retention daripada massive DELETE
```

---

# 4. Monitoring Table/Partition Stats

## 4.1 Per-partition activity

```sql id="x36cjy"
SELECT
    schemaname,
    relname AS partition_name,
    n_live_tup,
    n_dead_tup,
    n_tup_ins,
    n_tup_upd,
    n_tup_del,
    last_vacuum,
    last_autovacuum,
    last_analyze,
    last_autoanalyze,
    vacuum_count,
    autovacuum_count,
    analyze_count,
    autoanalyze_count
FROM pg_stat_user_tables
WHERE schemaname = 'app'
  AND relname LIKE 'audit_event_p%'
ORDER BY relname;
```

Interpretasi:

| Metric | Arti praktis |
|---|---|
| `n_live_tup` | Estimasi row hidup |
| `n_dead_tup` | Estimasi dead tuples |
| `n_tup_ins` | Insert activity |
| `n_tup_upd` | Update activity |
| `n_tup_del` | Delete activity |
| `last_autovacuum` | Kapan autovacuum terakhir |
| `last_autoanalyze` | Kapan autoanalyze terakhir |
| `autovacuum_count` | Seberapa sering autovacuum jalan |
| `autoanalyze_count` | Seberapa sering stats diperbarui |

PostgreSQL system catalogs menyimpan metadata database seperti table dan column; untuk observability runtime, gunakan statistics views seperti `pg_stat_user_tables`, sedangkan catalog seperti `pg_class`, `pg_inherits`, dan `pg_partitioned_table` berguna untuk metadata partition structure. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/catalogs.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 4.2 Dead tuple ratio

```sql id="9z5pc4"
SELECT
    relname AS partition_name,
    n_live_tup,
    n_dead_tup,
    ROUND(
        n_dead_tup::numeric
        / NULLIF(n_live_tup + n_dead_tup, 0),
        4
    ) AS dead_tuple_ratio,
    last_autovacuum,
    last_autoanalyze
FROM pg_stat_user_tables
WHERE schemaname = 'app'
  AND relname LIKE 'audit_event_p%'
ORDER BY dead_tuple_ratio DESC NULLS LAST;
```

Rule of thumb:

| Dead tuple ratio | Interpretasi |
|---:|---|
| `< 5%` | Biasanya sehat |
| `5-20%` | Perlu pantau |
| `> 20%` | Investigasi vacuum/update/delete pattern |
| Tinggi di hot partition | Normal-ish tapi perlu autovacuum tuning |
| Tinggi di cold partition | Ada delete/update tidak terduga atau vacuum belum jalan |

---

# 5. Size Monitoring per Partition

## 5.1 Table size, index size, total size

```sql id="opz55t"
SELECT
    child.oid::regclass AS partition_name,
    pg_size_pretty(pg_relation_size(child.oid)) AS table_size,
    pg_size_pretty(pg_indexes_size(child.oid)) AS index_size,
    pg_size_pretty(pg_total_relation_size(child.oid)) AS total_size,
    pg_relation_size(child.oid) AS table_size_bytes,
    pg_indexes_size(child.oid) AS index_size_bytes,
    pg_total_relation_size(child.oid) AS total_size_bytes
FROM pg_inherits i
JOIN pg_class parent ON parent.oid = i.inhparent
JOIN pg_class child  ON child.oid = i.inhrelid
WHERE parent.oid = 'app.audit_event'::regclass
ORDER BY pg_total_relation_size(child.oid) DESC;
```

## 5.2 Index-to-table ratio

```sql id="b9lsp4"
SELECT
    child.oid::regclass AS partition_name,
    pg_size_pretty(pg_relation_size(child.oid)) AS table_size,
    pg_size_pretty(pg_indexes_size(child.oid)) AS index_size,
    ROUND(
        pg_indexes_size(child.oid)::numeric
        / NULLIF(pg_relation_size(child.oid), 0),
        2
    ) AS index_to_table_ratio
FROM pg_inherits i
JOIN pg_class parent ON parent.oid = i.inhparent
JOIN pg_class child  ON child.oid = i.inhrelid
WHERE parent.oid = 'app.audit_event'::regclass
ORDER BY index_to_table_ratio DESC NULLS LAST;
```

Interpretasi:

| Ratio | Kemungkinan |
|---:|---|
| `< 0.5` | Index footprint rendah |
| `0.5-1.5` | Masih umum tergantung workload |
| `> 2.0` | Review index strategy |
| Sangat tinggi | Banyak index, GIN besar, bloat, atau table kecil/index banyak |

Jangan drop index hanya karena besar. Validasi dengan query pattern dan `pg_stat_user_indexes`.

---

# 6. Partition Size Skew

## 6.1 Apa itu skew?

Skew terjadi ketika satu partition jauh lebih besar dari yang lain.

```text id="uhm0z8"
audit_event_p2026_07 = 800 GB
audit_event_p2026_08 = 120 GB
audit_event_p2026_09 = 130 GB
```

Penyebab:

| Penyebab | Contoh |
|---|---|
| Traffic spike | Migrasi besar bulan tertentu |
| Tenant besar | Satu tenant menghasilkan mayoritas event |
| Late-arriving data | Data lama masuk ke partition tertentu |
| Boundary terlalu kasar | Monthly terlalu besar, perlu daily/subpartition |
| Default partition menumpuk | Future partition missing |
| Backfill tidak seimbang | Historical load tidak rata |

## 6.2 Detect size skew

```sql id="dmu86g"
WITH sizes AS (
    SELECT
        child.oid::regclass AS partition_name,
        pg_total_relation_size(child.oid) AS total_size_bytes
    FROM pg_inherits i
    JOIN pg_class parent ON parent.oid = i.inhparent
    JOIN pg_class child  ON child.oid = i.inhrelid
    WHERE parent.oid = 'app.audit_event'::regclass
)
SELECT
    partition_name,
    pg_size_pretty(total_size_bytes) AS total_size,
    ROUND(
        total_size_bytes::numeric
        / NULLIF(avg(total_size_bytes) OVER (), 0),
        2
    ) AS size_vs_avg
FROM sizes
ORDER BY total_size_bytes DESC;
```

Alert idea:

```text id="063oe4"
size_vs_avg > 3 untuk non-current partition
```

Current partition boleh berbeda jika sedang aktif.

---

# 7. Default Partition Monitoring

Default partition harus dianggap sebagai safety net.

## 7.1 Row count

```sql id="h81gl6"
SELECT COUNT(*) AS default_rows
FROM app.audit_event_default;
```

## 7.2 Size

```sql id="h57rpd"
SELECT
    pg_size_pretty(pg_total_relation_size('app.audit_event_default')) AS default_total_size,
    pg_total_relation_size('app.audit_event_default') AS default_total_size_bytes;
```

## 7.3 Boundary diagnosis

```sql id="q8p6gr"
SELECT
    min(event_time) AS min_event_time,
    max(event_time) AS max_event_time,
    count(*) AS row_count
FROM app.audit_event_default;
```

## 7.4 Group default rows by month

```sql id="5gck3u"
SELECT
    date_trunc('month', event_time) AS month_bucket,
    COUNT(*) AS row_count
FROM app.audit_event_default
GROUP BY date_trunc('month', event_time)
ORDER BY month_bucket;
```

Alert:

| Condition | Severity |
|---|---|
| `default_rows > 0` untuk table strict | Warning/critical |
| default rows growing | Critical |
| default size > threshold | Critical |
| default contains future date | Future partition missing |
| default contains ancient date | Late-arriving/archive rule issue |

---

# 8. Future Partition Horizon Monitoring

Untuk monthly partition dengan naming convention `audit_event_pYYYY_MM`.

```sql id="296ipz"
WITH partitions AS (
    SELECT
        child.relname AS partition_name,
        to_date(
            substring(child.relname FROM 'audit_event_p([0-9]{4}_[0-9]{2})'),
            'YYYY_MM'
        ) AS month_start
    FROM pg_inherits i
    JOIN pg_class parent ON parent.oid = i.inhparent
    JOIN pg_class child  ON child.oid = i.inhrelid
    WHERE parent.oid = 'app.audit_event'::regclass
      AND child.relname ~ '^audit_event_p[0-9]{4}_[0-9]{2}$'
)
SELECT
    max(month_start) AS latest_partition_month,
    (
        date_trunc('month', current_date)::date + interval '1 month'
    )::date AS next_required_month,
    (
        max(month_start)
        - date_trunc('month', current_date)::date
    ) AS horizon
FROM partitions;
```

Lebih baik untuk production: simpan metadata partition di table lifecycle, karena parsing nama bisa salah kalau naming berubah.

Alert idea:

| Partition interval | Alert jika horizon kurang dari |
|---|---:|
| Daily | 7 hari |
| Weekly | 4 minggu |
| Monthly | 3 bulan |
| Yearly | 1 tahun |

---

# 9. Catalog Query: Partition Structure

## 9.1 List parent and child partitions

```sql id="sge9vb"
SELECT
    parent.oid::regclass AS parent_table,
    child.oid::regclass AS child_partition
FROM pg_inherits i
JOIN pg_class parent ON parent.oid = i.inhparent
JOIN pg_class child  ON child.oid = i.inhrelid
WHERE parent.oid = 'app.audit_event'::regclass
ORDER BY child.oid::regclass::text;
```

## 9.2 Partition strategy and key

```sql id="crq65c"
SELECT
    c.oid::regclass AS table_name,
    pt.partstrat,
    pg_get_partkeydef(c.oid) AS partition_key
FROM pg_class c
JOIN pg_partitioned_table pt ON pt.partrelid = c.oid
WHERE c.oid = 'app.audit_event'::regclass;
```

Interpretasi `partstrat`:

```text id="icvp91"
r = range
l = list
h = hash
```

## 9.3 Partition bounds

```sql id="z3w02s"
SELECT
    c.oid::regclass AS partition_name,
    pg_get_expr(c.relpartbound, c.oid) AS partition_bound
FROM pg_class c
JOIN pg_inherits i ON i.inhrelid = c.oid
WHERE i.inhparent = 'app.audit_event'::regclass
ORDER BY c.oid::regclass::text;
```

---

# 10. Index Observability

## 10.1 Index usage per partition

```sql id="eo2fcb"
SELECT
    schemaname,
    relname AS partition_name,
    indexrelname AS index_name,
    idx_scan,
    idx_tup_read,
    idx_tup_fetch
FROM pg_stat_user_indexes
WHERE schemaname = 'app'
  AND relname LIKE 'audit_event_p%'
ORDER BY idx_scan ASC, relname, indexrelname;
```

Interpretasi:

| Metric | Arti |
|---|---|
| `idx_scan` | Berapa kali index dipakai |
| `idx_tup_read` | Entry index yang dibaca |
| `idx_tup_fetch` | Heap row yang diambil |
| `idx_scan = 0` | Belum tentu unused permanen; cek periode stats |
| High read/fetch | Index aktif |
| Low scan + huge size | Kandidat review |

## 10.2 Index size detail

```sql id="4e7o9w"
SELECT
    tablename AS partition_name,
    indexname,
    pg_size_pretty(pg_relation_size((schemaname || '.' || indexname)::regclass)) AS index_size,
    pg_relation_size((schemaname || '.' || indexname)::regclass) AS index_size_bytes,
    indexdef
FROM pg_indexes
WHERE schemaname = 'app'
  AND tablename LIKE 'audit_event_p%'
ORDER BY index_size_bytes DESC;
```

## 10.3 Missing index per partition

Misal semua partition harus punya `tenant_time_idx`.

```sql id="4b5xy1"
WITH partitions AS (
    SELECT child.relname AS partition_name
    FROM pg_inherits i
    JOIN pg_class parent ON parent.oid = i.inhparent
    JOIN pg_class child  ON child.oid = i.inhrelid
    WHERE parent.oid = 'app.audit_event'::regclass
      AND child.relname <> 'audit_event_default'
),
idx AS (
    SELECT
        tablename,
        indexname
    FROM pg_indexes
    WHERE schemaname = 'app'
      AND indexname LIKE '%tenant_time_idx'
)
SELECT p.partition_name
FROM partitions p
LEFT JOIN idx i ON i.tablename = p.partition_name
WHERE i.indexname IS NULL
ORDER BY p.partition_name;
```

---

# 11. Bloat Monitoring

## 11.1 Practical view using `pg_stat_user_tables`

Basic bloat indicator:

```sql id="f20ogw"
SELECT
    relname AS partition_name,
    n_live_tup,
    n_dead_tup,
    ROUND(
        n_dead_tup::numeric
        / NULLIF(n_live_tup + n_dead_tup, 0),
        4
    ) AS dead_tuple_ratio,
    pg_size_pretty(pg_total_relation_size(relid)) AS total_size
FROM pg_stat_user_tables
WHERE schemaname = 'app'
  AND relname LIKE 'audit_event_p%'
ORDER BY dead_tuple_ratio DESC NULLS LAST;
```

Ini bukan bloat measurement presisi, tetapi sangat berguna sebagai early warning.

## 11.2 `pgstattuple`

Untuk pengukuran lebih detail, PostgreSQL menyediakan extension `pgstattuple`, yang memberi statistik tuple-level dan page-level untuk table/index. Dokumentasi memperingatkan fungsi-fungsi ini membaca data secara detail sehingga bisa mahal pada object besar. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/pgstattuple.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Enable:

```sql id="72y3qo"
CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pgstattuple;
```

Table bloat sample:

```sql id="iohzxp"
SELECT *
FROM pgstattuple('app.audit_event_p2026_07');
```

Index bloat sample:

```sql id="enp7dz"
SELECT *
FROM pgstatindex('app.audit_event_p2026_07_tenant_time_idx');
```

Practical rule:

```text id="6o540p"
Jangan jalankan pgstattuple sembarangan pada semua partition besar di jam sibuk.
Gunakan sampling, maintenance window, atau target partition yang dicurigai.
```

---

# 12. Autovacuum Tuning per Partition

## 12.1 Kapan perlu tuning?

Tuning per table/partition layak jika:

```text id="dp5d9k"
- hot partition punya n_dead_tup tinggi terus
- outbox/inbox banyak update status
- autovacuum terlambat
- query mulai lambat karena bloat/stale stats
- replica/WAL pressure masih acceptable
```

## 12.2 Example tuning hot outbox partition

```sql id="vtrh5i"
ALTER TABLE app.outbox_event_p2026_07
SET (
    autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor = 0.02,
    autovacuum_analyze_scale_factor = 0.01,
    autovacuum_vacuum_cost_limit = 2000
);
```

## 12.3 Reset tuning

```sql id="in7ftf"
ALTER TABLE app.outbox_event_p2026_07
RESET (
    autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor,
    autovacuum_analyze_scale_factor,
    autovacuum_vacuum_cost_limit
);
```

Caution:

```text id="2vnh94"
Autovacuum tuning tidak boleh asal agresif.
Terlalu agresif bisa menambah I/O pressure.
Terlalu lemah bisa membuat bloat dan stale stats.
```

---

# 13. Statistics Freshness

## 13.1 Cek stale analyze

```sql id="tdbk6p"
SELECT
    relname AS partition_name,
    n_tup_ins,
    n_tup_upd,
    n_tup_del,
    last_analyze,
    last_autoanalyze,
    now() - GREATEST(
        COALESCE(last_analyze, 'epoch'::timestamptz),
        COALESCE(last_autoanalyze, 'epoch'::timestamptz)
    ) AS analyze_age
FROM pg_stat_user_tables
WHERE schemaname = 'app'
  AND relname LIKE 'audit_event_p%'
ORDER BY analyze_age DESC;
```

## 13.2 Manual analyze after attach/backfill

```sql id="hjq185"
ANALYZE app.audit_event_p2026_07;
```

## 13.3 Parent analyze?

Analyze parent and partitions if needed:

```sql id="9ftm3f"
ANALYZE app.audit_event;
```

For critical query plan validation, still check `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` after backfill/attach.

---

# 14. Query Observability dengan `pg_stat_statements`

`pg_stat_statements` melacak statistik planning dan execution dari SQL yang dijalankan server, dan perlu dimuat lewat `shared_preload_libraries` lalu extension dibuat di database. Ini sangat berguna untuk menemukan query partitioned table yang lambat atau tidak partition-friendly. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/routine-vacuuming.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 14.1 Top expensive queries

```sql id="0bd8ko"
SELECT
    calls,
    ROUND(total_exec_time::numeric, 2) AS total_exec_time_ms,
    ROUND(mean_exec_time::numeric, 2) AS mean_exec_time_ms,
    ROUND(max_exec_time::numeric, 2) AS max_exec_time_ms,
    rows,
    query
FROM pg_stat_statements
WHERE query ILIKE '%audit_event%'
ORDER BY total_exec_time DESC
LIMIT 30;
```

## 14.2 Heuristic: query tanpa partition key

```sql id="dfupxu"
SELECT
    calls,
    ROUND(total_exec_time::numeric, 2) AS total_exec_time_ms,
    ROUND(mean_exec_time::numeric, 2) AS mean_exec_time_ms,
    query
FROM pg_stat_statements
WHERE query ILIKE '%audit_event%'
  AND query NOT ILIKE '%event_time%'
ORDER BY total_exec_time DESC
LIMIT 30;
```

Catatan:

```text id="6b3bx0"
Ini heuristic text-based.
Query bisa memakai alias, generated SQL, atau parameter naming berbeda.
Tetap kombinasikan dengan mapper review dan EXPLAIN.
```

---

# 15. EXPLAIN Baseline Monitoring

Untuk endpoint penting, simpan baseline plan.

## 15.1 Tenant timeline query

```sql id="9s5p8d"
EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS, FORMAT JSON)
SELECT
    event_id,
    tenant_id,
    case_id,
    event_time,
    event_type
FROM app.audit_event
WHERE tenant_id = '00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001'
  AND event_time >= TIMESTAMPTZ '2026-07-01 00:00:00+00'
  AND event_time <  TIMESTAMPTZ '2026-08-01 00:00:00+00'
ORDER BY event_time DESC
LIMIT 100;
```

## 15.2 Baseline assertion

```text id="i50jsy"
Expected:
- only audit_event_p2026_07
- Index Scan using tenant_time_idx
- no full Append all partitions
- no external sort
- execution below threshold
```

PostgreSQL docs emphasize that choosing the right query plan is critical, and `EXPLAIN` is the tool to inspect the plan PostgreSQL chooses. For partitioning, this is the practical proof that pruning/index strategy still works. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/using-explain.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 16. Lock and Long Transaction Monitoring

## 16.1 Long transactions

```sql id="z4lfxo"
SELECT
    pid,
    usename,
    application_name,
    state,
    now() - xact_start AS xact_age,
    wait_event_type,
    wait_event,
    left(query, 500) AS query
FROM pg_stat_activity
WHERE xact_start IS NOT NULL
ORDER BY xact_age DESC;
```

Long transactions can prevent vacuum cleanup and cause bloat pressure.

## 16.2 Blocking sessions

```sql id="27l4pn"
SELECT
    blocked.pid AS blocked_pid,
    blocked_activity.usename AS blocked_user,
    blocked_activity.application_name AS blocked_app,
    blocked_activity.query AS blocked_query,
    blocking.pid AS blocking_pid,
    blocking_activity.usename AS blocking_user,
    blocking_activity.application_name AS blocking_app,
    blocking_activity.query AS blocking_query
FROM pg_locks blocked
JOIN pg_stat_activity blocked_activity
  ON blocked_activity.pid = blocked.pid
JOIN pg_locks blocking
  ON blocking.locktype = blocked.locktype
 AND blocking.database IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked.database
 AND blocking.relation IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked.relation
 AND blocking.page IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked.page
 AND blocking.tuple IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked.tuple
 AND blocking.virtualxid IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked.virtualxid
 AND blocking.transactionid IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked.transactionid
 AND blocking.classid IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked.classid
 AND blocking.objid IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked.objid
 AND blocking.objsubid IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked.objsubid
 AND blocking.pid <> blocked.pid
JOIN pg_stat_activity blocking_activity
  ON blocking_activity.pid = blocking.pid
WHERE NOT blocked.granted
  AND blocking.granted;
```

Use case:

```text id="4btyof"
- attach partition waiting
- detach/drop partition waiting
- index build waiting
- retention job stuck
- app query blocked by migration
```

---

# 17. Progress Monitoring

PostgreSQL menyediakan progress reporting untuk beberapa command, termasuk `CREATE INDEX`, `VACUUM`, dan lainnya. Ini penting saat maintenance berjalan lama. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/progress-reporting.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 17.1 Create index progress

```sql id="7acgdm"
SELECT
    pid,
    datname,
    relid::regclass AS table_name,
    index_relid::regclass AS index_name,
    command,
    phase,
    lockers_total,
    lockers_done,
    blocks_total,
    blocks_done,
    tuples_total,
    tuples_done
FROM pg_stat_progress_create_index;
```

## 17.2 Vacuum progress

```sql id="000fir"
SELECT
    pid,
    datname,
    relid::regclass AS table_name,
    phase,
    heap_blks_total,
    heap_blks_scanned,
    heap_blks_vacuumed,
    index_vacuum_count,
    max_dead_tuple_bytes,
    dead_tuple_bytes
FROM pg_stat_progress_vacuum;
```

Use case:

```text id="mh8aj9"
Ketika VACUUM manual atau autovacuum besar berjalan,
cek partition mana yang sedang diproses dan progress phase-nya.
```

---

# 18. Retention Job Observability

Jika Anda punya table lifecycle metadata:

```sql id="d3ql47"
CREATE TABLE app.partition_lifecycle_event (
    lifecycle_event_id uuid        NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    table_name         text        NOT NULL,
    partition_name     text        NOT NULL,
    action             text        NOT NULL,
    action_status      text        NOT NULL,
    lower_bound        timestamptz,
    upper_bound        timestamptz,
    row_count          bigint,
    total_size_bytes   bigint,
    archive_location   text,
    executed_by        text        NOT NULL,
    executed_at        timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    details            jsonb       NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    CONSTRAINT partition_lifecycle_event_pk
        PRIMARY KEY (lifecycle_event_id)
);
```

## 18.1 Recent lifecycle events

```sql id="32devp"
SELECT
    table_name,
    partition_name,
    action,
    action_status,
    lower_bound,
    upper_bound,
    row_count,
    pg_size_pretty(total_size_bytes) AS total_size,
    archive_location,
    executed_by,
    executed_at,
    details
FROM app.partition_lifecycle_event
WHERE table_name = 'audit_event'
ORDER BY executed_at DESC
LIMIT 50;
```

## 18.2 Failed lifecycle actions

```sql id="9jnyxz"
SELECT *
FROM app.partition_lifecycle_event
WHERE action_status = 'FAILED'
ORDER BY executed_at DESC;
```

## 18.3 Retention lag

```sql id="dwywqx"
WITH latest_drop AS (
    SELECT max(upper_bound) AS latest_dropped_until
    FROM app.partition_lifecycle_event
    WHERE table_name = 'audit_event'
      AND action IN ('DROP', 'ARCHIVE')
      AND action_status = 'SUCCESS'
)
SELECT
    latest_dropped_until,
    now() - latest_dropped_until AS retention_lag
FROM latest_drop;
```

---

# 19. Java/Jersey Internal Health Endpoint

## 19.1 Endpoint

```http id="t1uxuc"
GET /internal/db/partition-health/audit-event
```

## 19.2 Response example

```json id="zvsj04"
{
  "table": "app.audit_event",
  "partitionKey": "RANGE(event_time)",
  "partitionCount": 42,
  "currentPartitionExists": true,
  "futurePartitionHorizonDays": 168,
  "defaultPartitionRows": 0,
  "largestPartition": {
    "name": "audit_event_p2026_07",
    "totalSizeBytes": 91234567890,
    "estimatedRows": 41234567
  },
  "staleAnalyzePartitions": [
    "audit_event_p2026_06"
  ],
  "highDeadTuplePartitions": [],
  "missingIndexes": [],
  "alerts": []
}
```

## 19.3 Mapper query example

```xml id="7zl6cr"
<select id="listPartitionStats" resultType="PartitionStat">
    SELECT
        child.relname AS partition_name,
        pg_total_relation_size(child.oid) AS total_size_bytes,
        pg_relation_size(child.oid) AS table_size_bytes,
        pg_indexes_size(child.oid) AS index_size_bytes,
        stats.n_live_tup,
        stats.n_dead_tup,
        stats.last_autovacuum,
        stats.last_autoanalyze
    FROM pg_inherits i
    JOIN pg_class parent ON parent.oid = i.inhparent
    JOIN pg_class child  ON child.oid = i.inhrelid
    LEFT JOIN pg_stat_user_tables stats
      ON stats.relid = child.oid
    WHERE parent.oid = #{parentTable}::regclass
    ORDER BY child.relname
</select>
```

## 19.4 Service evaluation logic

```java id="u56jkj"
public List<String> evaluateAlerts(List<PartitionStat> partitions, long defaultRows) {
    List<String> alerts = new ArrayList<>();

    if (defaultRows > 0) {
        alerts.add("Default partition contains rows");
    }

    for (PartitionStat p : partitions) {
        long total = p.liveTuples() + p.deadTuples();
        if (total > 0) {
            double deadRatio = (double) p.deadTuples() / total;
            if (deadRatio > 0.20) {
                alerts.add("High dead tuple ratio: " + p.partitionName());
            }
        }

        if (p.lastAutoanalyze() == null) {
            alerts.add("Partition has never been autoanalyzed: " + p.partitionName());
        }
    }

    return alerts;
}
```

---

# 20. Alerting Rules

## 20.1 Core alerts

| Alert | Condition | Severity |
|---|---|---|
| Missing current partition | Current date not covered | Critical |
| Low future horizon | Future partition < threshold | Warning/Critical |
| Default partition rows | `default_rows > 0` | Warning/Critical |
| Default partition growing | row count increasing | Critical |
| High dead tuple ratio | `dead_tuple_ratio > 20%` | Warning |
| Stale analyze | no analyze after large load | Warning |
| Partition size skew | size > 3x avg | Warning |
| Missing required index | index absent on active partition | Critical |
| Long transaction | xact age > threshold | Warning/Critical |
| Blocking DDL | lock wait on partition parent | Critical |
| Failed retention job | lifecycle status failed | Critical |
| Replication lag during backfill | lag > threshold | Warning/Critical |

## 20.2 Threshold starting point

```text id="59j7dx"
Monthly partition:
- future horizon < 3 months = warning
- future horizon < 1 month = critical

Daily partition:
- future horizon < 7 days = warning
- future horizon < 2 days = critical

Default partition:
- rows > 0 = warning for audit/history
- rows growing for 2 intervals = critical
```

Tune based on business SLA.

---

# 21. Maintenance Runbooks

## 21.1 Runbook: Default Partition Has Rows

```text id="9wncxf"
Symptoms:
- default_rows > 0
- inserts still succeeding
- query performance may degrade later

Steps:
1. Check min/max partition key in default.
2. Group rows by month/day.
3. Identify missing target partition.
4. Create correct partition if missing.
5. Move rows from default to staging/target.
6. Validate row count.
7. Delete moved rows from default.
8. ANALYZE affected partitions.
9. Record incident/lifecycle event.
10. Add alert/fix future partition job.
```

Diagnosis SQL:

```sql id="s7qqet"
SELECT
    date_trunc('month', event_time) AS month_bucket,
    COUNT(*) AS row_count
FROM app.audit_event_default
GROUP BY date_trunc('month', event_time)
ORDER BY month_bucket;
```

---

## 21.2 Runbook: Query Suddenly Scans All Partitions

```text id="t40005"
Symptoms:
- endpoint latency spikes
- EXPLAIN shows Append/Merge Append across many partitions

Steps:
1. Capture final SQL from app/log.
2. Check whether partition key predicate exists.
3. Check if function wraps partition key.
4. Check parameter type from MyBatis.
5. Run EXPLAIN ANALYZE BUFFERS.
6. Compare with baseline plan.
7. Roll back mapper change or enforce API validation.
8. Add regression EXPLAIN test.
```

Common fixes:

```text id="1pkfhx"
- Require from/to in API
- Replace date(event_time) with range predicate
- Ensure OffsetDateTime/Instant binding
- Add missing partition index if pruning is already OK
```

---

## 21.3 Runbook: High Dead Tuples on Hot Partition

```text id="kqqirl"
Symptoms:
- n_dead_tup high
- dead_tuple_ratio high
- autovacuum frequent or absent
- table/index size growing

Steps:
1. Identify write pattern: update/delete-heavy?
2. Check last_autovacuum/last_autoanalyze.
3. Check long transactions blocking cleanup.
4. Run manual VACUUM if needed.
5. Tune autovacuum for hot partition if recurring.
6. Review update-heavy design.
7. Review index count/write amplification.
```

SQL:

```sql id="x8bbhl"
VACUUM (VERBOSE, ANALYZE) app.outbox_event_p2026_07;
```

---

## 21.4 Runbook: Partition Size Skew

```text id="tjp1h6"
Symptoms:
- one partition 3x+ average
- query/maintenance slow on one partition

Steps:
1. Identify whether partition is current/historical/default.
2. Check data distribution by tenant/case/event_type.
3. Check late-arriving/backfill event.
4. Consider daily partition for future periods.
5. Consider subpartitioning only after benchmark.
6. For historical partition, archive/detach if eligible.
```

Distribution query:

```sql id="ng7flp"
SELECT
    tenant_id,
    COUNT(*) AS row_count,
    ROUND(COUNT(*) * 100.0 / SUM(COUNT(*)) OVER (), 2) AS pct
FROM app.audit_event_p2026_07
GROUP BY tenant_id
ORDER BY row_count DESC
LIMIT 20;
```

---

## 21.5 Runbook: Missing Index on New Partition

```text id="yen9pv"
Symptoms:
- new month starts, query gets slow
- EXPLAIN shows Seq Scan on new partition
- old partitions use Index Scan

Steps:
1. Verify index list on new partition.
2. Create missing index.
3. ANALYZE partition.
4. Verify EXPLAIN.
5. Fix future partition creation template.
```

SQL:

```sql id="ge3sfl"
CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS audit_event_p2026_08_tenant_time_idx
ON app.audit_event_p2026_08 (tenant_id, event_time DESC);

ANALYZE app.audit_event_p2026_08;
```

---

# 22. Maintenance Frequency Recommendation

| Task | Suggested frequency |
|---|---|
| Default partition row check | Every 5-15 minutes |
| Future horizon check | Daily |
| Partition size report | Daily |
| Dead tuple ratio report | Hourly/daily depending workload |
| Index usage review | Weekly/monthly |
| EXPLAIN baseline for critical endpoints | On release + periodic |
| Retention job | Daily/monthly depending interval |
| Stale analyze check | After bulk load + daily |
| Bloat deep inspection | Monthly or incident-driven |
| Long transaction alert | Continuous |
| Lock wait alert | Continuous |
| Backfill progress check | During migration |

---

# 23. Practical Dashboard Sections

Untuk Grafana/internal dashboard, pisahkan menjadi:

```text id="5w11vg"
1. Partition inventory
   - parent table
   - partition count
   - strategy/key
   - newest/oldest partition

2. Capacity
   - total size
   - size per partition
   - index/table ratio
   - growth rate

3. Health
   - default rows
   - future horizon
   - dead tuple ratio
   - last vacuum/analyze

4. Query performance
   - top pg_stat_statements
   - mean/max exec time
   - query without partition key heuristic

5. Lifecycle
   - retention job status
   - archive/drop events
   - failed lifecycle events

6. Operations
   - lock waits
   - long transactions
   - create index/vacuum progress
```

---

# 24. Common Anti-patterns

| Anti-pattern | Kenapa buruk |
|---|---|
| Tidak memonitor default partition | Missing future partition tidak terlihat |
| Hanya monitor parent table size | Problem ada di child partition |
| Tidak `ANALYZE` setelah attach/backfill | Planner bisa memilih plan buruk |
| Membuat future partition tanpa index | Bulan baru tiba-tiba lambat |
| Mengandalkan autovacuum tanpa observability | Tidak tahu kapan gagal/telat |
| Drop index karena `idx_scan=0` tanpa konteks | Stats bisa baru reset atau workload belum terjadi |
| Menjalankan `pgstattuple` semua partition besar di jam sibuk | Bisa mahal |
| Retention via massive DELETE | Dead tuples/WAL/vacuum pressure |
| Tidak ada lock_timeout migration | DDL bisa menggantung/block production |
| Tidak punya EXPLAIN baseline | Regression query tidak terdeteksi |

---

# 25. Production Checklist

| Checklist | Status |
|---|---|
| Query list partition parent/child tersedia |
| Size per partition dimonitor |
| Index size per partition dimonitor |
| Dead tuple ratio dimonitor |
| Last vacuum/analyze dimonitor |
| Default partition row count dimonitor |
| Future partition horizon dimonitor |
| Missing index per partition dideteksi |
| Query tanpa partition key dideteksi |
| EXPLAIN baseline disimpan |
| Retention lifecycle event dicatat |
| Failed retention job alert tersedia |
| Long transaction alert tersedia |
| Lock wait alert tersedia |
| Progress create index/vacuum bisa dicek |
| Bloat inspection process tersedia |
| Java/Jersey health endpoint tersedia |
| Runbook default partition tersedia |
| Runbook query scans all partitions tersedia |
| Runbook high bloat/dead tuples tersedia |

# 26. Kesimpulan Seri 12

Maintenance partitioned table harus berbasis **per-partition observability**, bukan hanya melihat parent table.

Rule paling penting:

```text id="8hn8p0"
1. Monitor child partitions, bukan hanya parent table.
2. Default partition harus kosong atau punya alasan jelas.
3. Future partition horizon harus selalu aman.
4. Partition baru harus punya index dan stats.
5. Setelah bulk load/attach, jalankan ANALYZE.
6. Dead tuple ratio dan bloat harus dipantau terutama di hot partition.
7. Query performance harus dikaitkan ke EXPLAIN dan pg_stat_statements.
8. Retention harus tercatat dan auditable.
9. Long transaction dan lock wait bisa merusak vacuum/DDL.
10. Semua alert harus punya runbook tindakan.
```

# Seri 13/14 - Automation & Platform Ops: pg_partman, pg_cron, Kubernetes CronJob, AWS/Azure/On-prem, dan Migration Pipeline

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini fokus pada automation partition lifecycle:

> **Bagaimana memastikan partition dibuat, di-index, dianalisis, dimonitor, di-retain, dan di-archive secara konsisten tanpa operasi manual yang rawan lupa?**

Setelah partitioning masuk production, masalah terbesar biasanya bukan syntax SQL, tetapi **operational drift**:

```text
- future partition lupa dibuat
- default partition diam-diam terisi
- partition baru tidak punya index
- ANALYZE lupa setelah attach/backfill
- retention job gagal tapi tidak ada alert
- migration DDL blocking traffic
- AWS/Azure/on-prem punya batasan extension berbeda
- pipeline GitHub/Maven tidak menguji insert routing/pruning
```

Untuk automation, ada dua pendekatan utama:

```text
1. Extension-driven automation
   - pg_partman
   - pg_cron
   - managed service scheduler support

2. Application/platform-driven automation
   - GitHub-reviewed migration
   - Kubernetes CronJob
   - Java maintenance service
   - PL/pgSQL function custom
   - external orchestrator
```

`pg_partman` adalah extension PostgreSQL untuk membuat dan mengelola partition set berbasis waktu maupun angka. Sejak pg_partman 5.x, extension ini fokus pada built-in declarative partitioning PostgreSQL dan metode lama berbasis trigger sudah deprecated. ([GitHub](https://github.com/pgpartman/pg_partman?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 2. Mental Model Automation

```text
Partition Automation
│
├─ 1. Provisioning
│  ├─ create parent table
│  ├─ create initial partitions
│  ├─ create future partitions
│  ├─ create indexes
│  └─ create default partition policy
│
├─ 2. Runtime Maintenance
│  ├─ create next partitions
│  ├─ run ANALYZE
│  ├─ check default partition
│  ├─ check missing indexes
│  └─ check future horizon
│
├─ 3. Retention
│  ├─ identify old partitions
│  ├─ legal hold check
│  ├─ detach/drop/archive
│  ├─ verify archive/checksum
│  └─ record lifecycle event
│
├─ 4. Observability
│  ├─ partition count
│  ├─ partition size
│  ├─ default rows
│  ├─ future horizon
│  ├─ dead tuples
│  ├─ failed jobs
│  └─ lock/long transaction
│
└─ 5. Platform Integration
   ├─ GitHub PR review
   ├─ Maven/Flyway/Liquibase
   ├─ Kubernetes CronJob
   ├─ AWS RDS/Aurora
   ├─ Azure Database for PostgreSQL
   └─ on-prem PostgreSQL
```

Core principle:

```text
Partition lifecycle must be:
- repeatable
- idempotent
- observable
- auditable
- rollback-aware
- environment-aware
```

---

# 3. Automation Option Matrix

| Option | Cocok untuk | Kelebihan | Risiko |
|---|---|---|---|
| Manual Git-reviewed SQL | Compliance ketat, partition bulanan, change control kuat | Explicit, auditable | Bisa lupa jika tidak ada alert |
| PL/pgSQL custom function | On-prem/custom policy, legal hold, audit metadata | Fleksibel | Anda maintain logic sendiri |
| Kubernetes CronJob | Microservice/K8s ops, hybrid platform | Portable, observable via platform | Credential/network/secrets harus aman |
| Java maintenance service | Butuh integration dengan domain/legal hold/app config | Bisa reusable dan testable | Menambah service responsibility |
| `pg_partman` | Banyak time-based/serial partition set | Battle-tested automation | Extension availability/config governance |
| `pg_cron` | Scheduler di dalam DB | Simple periodic SQL | Extension/permission constraints |
| Cloud-native scheduler | AWS EventBridge, Azure Automation, CI scheduler | Di luar DB, mudah audit | Network/credential/latency dependency |

AWS RDS documentation menjelaskan bahwa maintenance pg_partman dapat menjalankan pembuatan partition baru, detach partition, atau remove old partition melalui `run_maintenance_proc`, dan sering dijadwalkan dengan `pg_cron`. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/PostgreSQL_Partitions.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

# 4. Option A - Manual Git-reviewed Partition Migration

## 4.1 Kapan Cocok

Gunakan manual SQL jika:

```text
- partition interval monthly/yearly
- jumlah partition tidak terlalu banyak
- compliance/change control ketat
- semua DDL harus lewat GitHub PR
- DBA/platform team ingin approval eksplisit
```

## 4.2 Struktur Migration

```text
db/
├─ migration/
│  ├─ V20260716_001__create_audit_event_parent.sql
│  ├─ V20260716_002__create_audit_event_partitions_2026_q3.sql
│  ├─ V20260716_003__create_audit_event_indexes.sql
│  └─ V20260716_004__create_partition_health_views.sql
│
├─ repeatable/
│  └─ R__audit_event_future_partitions.sql
│
└─ checks/
   ├─ check_partition_horizon.sql
   ├─ check_default_partition.sql
   └─ check_missing_partition_indexes.sql
```

## 4.3 Idempotent Future Partition Script

```sql
BEGIN;

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS app.audit_event_p2026_10
PARTITION OF app.audit_event
FOR VALUES FROM ('2026-10-01 00:00:00+00')
             TO ('2026-11-01 00:00:00+00');

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS audit_event_p2026_10_tenant_time_idx
ON app.audit_event_p2026_10 (tenant_id, event_time DESC);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS audit_event_p2026_10_case_time_idx
ON app.audit_event_p2026_10 (case_id, event_time DESC);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS audit_event_p2026_10_event_type_time_idx
ON app.audit_event_p2026_10 (event_type, event_time DESC);

ANALYZE app.audit_event_p2026_10;

COMMIT;
```

## 4.4 GitHub PR Checklist

```text
DDL PR must prove:
1. Partition bounds do not overlap
2. No boundary gap
3. Future horizon is sufficient
4. Required indexes exist
5. Insert routing smoke test passes
6. EXPLAIN pruning test passes
7. Default partition policy is unchanged or explicitly reviewed
8. Rollback plan exists
```

---

# 5. Option B - Custom PL/pgSQL Automation

## 5.1 Kapan Cocok

Gunakan custom function jika lifecycle punya aturan domain:

```text
- legal hold
- regulatory retention
- archive verification
- custom lifecycle metadata
- approval/check ticket
- table-specific index template
```

## 5.2 Metadata Table

```sql
CREATE TABLE app.partition_lifecycle_config (
    table_name              text        NOT NULL,
    schema_name             text        NOT NULL,
    partition_key           text        NOT NULL,
    partition_interval      text        NOT NULL,
    future_horizon_months   integer     NOT NULL,
    retention_months        integer,
    default_partition_name  text,
    enabled                 boolean     NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
    created_at              timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at              timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    CONSTRAINT partition_lifecycle_config_pk
        PRIMARY KEY (schema_name, table_name),

    CONSTRAINT partition_interval_chk
        CHECK (partition_interval IN ('daily', 'monthly', 'yearly'))
);
```

Example config:

```sql
INSERT INTO app.partition_lifecycle_config (
    schema_name,
    table_name,
    partition_key,
    partition_interval,
    future_horizon_months,
    retention_months,
    default_partition_name
)
VALUES (
    'app',
    'audit_event',
    'event_time',
    'monthly',
    6,
    36,
    'audit_event_default'
);
```

---

## 5.3 Function: Create Monthly Partition

```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION app.ensure_audit_event_month_partition(
    p_month_start timestamptz
)
RETURNS void
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_month_start timestamptz;
    v_month_end   timestamptz;
    v_table_name  text;
BEGIN
    v_month_start := date_trunc('month', p_month_start);
    v_month_end   := v_month_start + interval '1 month';

    v_table_name := format(
        'audit_event_p%s',
        to_char(v_month_start, 'YYYY_MM')
    );

    EXECUTE format(
        'CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS app.%I
         PARTITION OF app.audit_event
         FOR VALUES FROM (%L) TO (%L)',
        v_table_name,
        v_month_start,
        v_month_end
    );

    EXECUTE format(
        'CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS %I
         ON app.%I (tenant_id, event_time DESC)',
        v_table_name || '_tenant_time_idx',
        v_table_name
    );

    EXECUTE format(
        'CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS %I
         ON app.%I (case_id, event_time DESC)',
        v_table_name || '_case_time_idx',
        v_table_name
    );

    EXECUTE format(
        'CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS %I
         ON app.%I (event_type, event_time DESC)',
        v_table_name || '_event_type_time_idx',
        v_table_name
    );

    EXECUTE format('ANALYZE app.%I', v_table_name);
END;
$$;
```

## 5.4 Function: Ensure Future Horizon

```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION app.ensure_audit_event_future_partitions(
    p_months_ahead integer DEFAULT 6
)
RETURNS void
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_i integer;
    v_month_start timestamptz;
BEGIN
    FOR v_i IN 0..p_months_ahead LOOP
        v_month_start := date_trunc('month', now()) + (v_i || ' months')::interval;
        PERFORM app.ensure_audit_event_month_partition(v_month_start);
    END LOOP;
END;
$$;
```

Run manually:

```sql
SELECT app.ensure_audit_event_future_partitions(6);
```

## 5.5 Guardrail: Log Lifecycle Event

```sql
CREATE TABLE app.partition_lifecycle_event (
    lifecycle_event_id uuid        NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    table_name         text        NOT NULL,
    partition_name     text        NOT NULL,
    action             text        NOT NULL,
    action_status      text        NOT NULL,
    lower_bound        timestamptz,
    upper_bound        timestamptz,
    executed_by        text        NOT NULL,
    executed_at        timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    details            jsonb       NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,

    CONSTRAINT partition_lifecycle_event_pk
        PRIMARY KEY (lifecycle_event_id)
);
```

Add inside function after partition creation if you want full audit trail.

---

# 6. Option C - pg_partman

## 6.1 Apa itu pg_partman?

`pg_partman` adalah PostgreSQL extension untuk membuat dan mengelola partition set berbasis waktu maupun angka. Ia membantu otomatisasi pembuatan child partitions dan retention policy. pg_partman 5.x mendukung built-in declarative partitioning; metode lama trigger-based sudah deprecated. ([GitHub](https://github.com/pgpartman/pg_partman?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Cocok jika:

```text
- banyak tabel partitioned
- interval harian/bulanan rutin
- ingin automatic premake future partitions
- retention policy sederhana
- extension allowed di environment
```

Kurang cocok jika:

```text
- compliance mengharuskan semua DDL manual PR
- retention perlu legal hold kompleks
- cloud environment tidak enable extension
- tim belum siap memahami part_config/run_maintenance behavior
```

---

## 6.2 Setup Extension

```sql
CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS partman;

CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_partman
WITH SCHEMA partman;
```

Catatan: permission dan extension availability berbeda di on-prem, AWS RDS/Aurora, dan Azure Database for PostgreSQL. Di managed service, extension harus tersedia dan user/role harus punya privilege yang cukup.

---

## 6.3 Contoh Parent Table

```sql
CREATE TABLE app.audit_event (
    event_id    uuid        NOT NULL,
    tenant_id   uuid        NOT NULL,
    case_id     uuid        NOT NULL,
    event_time  timestamptz NOT NULL,
    event_type  text        NOT NULL,
    payload     jsonb       NOT NULL,

    CONSTRAINT audit_event_pk
        PRIMARY KEY (event_id, event_time)
) PARTITION BY RANGE (event_time);
```

## 6.4 Register Parent ke pg_partman

Bentuk persis parameter dapat berbeda antar versi pg_partman; validasi dengan dokumentasi versi extension yang terpasang. Pola umumnya:

```sql
SELECT partman.create_parent(
    p_parent_table => 'app.audit_event',
    p_control      => 'event_time',
    p_type         => 'range',
    p_interval     => 'monthly',
    p_premake      => 6
);
```

Azure documentation menjelaskan bahwa `create_parent()` mengisi `part_config` dan `part_config_sub`; maintenance function kemudian memeriksa konfigurasi tersebut untuk membuat partition baru atau menjalankan retention policy. ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/postgresql/configure-maintain/how-to-use-pg-partman?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 6.5 Maintenance Function

Common maintenance call:

```sql
CALL partman.run_maintenance_proc();
```

atau tergantung versi/instalasi:

```sql
SELECT partman.run_maintenance();
```

AWS RDS documentation menjelaskan penggunaan `run_maintenance_proc` dari `pg_partman` bersama `pg_cron` untuk otomatis membuat partition baru, detach partition, atau remove old partitions. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/PostgreSQL_Partitions.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 6.6 Retention Configuration

Contoh update config:

```sql
UPDATE partman.part_config
SET
    retention = '36 months',
    retention_keep_table = true
WHERE parent_table = 'app.audit_event';
```

Interpretasi policy harus dicek sesuai versi pg_partman:

```text
retention_keep_table = true
  biasanya berarti partition lama di-detach/dipertahankan sebagai table

retention_keep_table = false
  dapat berarti table lama di-drop sesuai policy
```

Jangan aktifkan drop otomatis untuk data regulatory/audit tanpa legal hold dan archive verification.

---

## 6.7 pg_partman Operational Checks

```sql
SELECT *
FROM partman.part_config
WHERE parent_table = 'app.audit_event';
```

Cek child partitions:

```sql
SELECT
    parent.oid::regclass AS parent_table,
    child.oid::regclass AS partition_table
FROM pg_inherits i
JOIN pg_class parent ON parent.oid = i.inhparent
JOIN pg_class child ON child.oid = i.inhrelid
WHERE parent.oid = 'app.audit_event'::regclass
ORDER BY child.oid::regclass::text;
```

---

## 6.8 pg_partman Failure Modes

| Failure mode | Gejala | Mitigasi |
|---|---|---|
| Maintenance job tidak jalan | Future partition habis | Monitor horizon + failed scheduler |
| Config salah | Partition interval/boundary salah | Review `part_config` |
| Retention terlalu agresif | Partition lama detach/drop terlalu cepat | Disable auto drop untuk regulated data |
| Index template tidak sesuai | New partition lambat | Verify index creation behavior |
| Permission issue | job gagal | Dedicated role + alert |
| Default partition tetap terisi | future creation tidak cukup | Monitor default rows |
| Version mismatch | function signature beda | Pin/check extension version per env |

---

# 7. Option D - pg_cron Scheduler

## 7.1 Apa itu pg_cron?

`pg_cron` adalah extension scheduler PostgreSQL yang dapat menjalankan SQL command secara periodik di dalam database. AWS RDS documentation menyebut pg_cron didukung pada RDS for PostgreSQL engine version 12.5 ke atas. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/PostgreSQL_pg_cron.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 7.2 Setup

```sql
CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_cron;
```

Managed service mungkin memerlukan parameter group/server parameter khusus dan restart. Ikuti dokumentasi provider.

## 7.3 Schedule pg_partman Maintenance

Contoh jadwal hourly:

```sql
SELECT cron.schedule(
    'pg_partman_maintenance_hourly',
    '0 * * * *',
    $$CALL partman.run_maintenance_proc();$$
);
```

AWS RDS documentation memberi pola bahwa `pg_cron` dapat menjadwalkan maintenance command/function/procedure di database, termasuk maintenance partition dengan pg_partman. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/PostgreSQL_Partitions.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 7.4 Schedule Custom Function

```sql
SELECT cron.schedule(
    'audit_event_future_partitions_daily',
    '5 0 * * *',
    $$SELECT app.ensure_audit_event_future_partitions(6);$$
);
```

## 7.5 Cek Cron Jobs

```sql
SELECT *
FROM cron.job
ORDER BY jobid;
```

Cek run detail jika tersedia pada versi/instalasi Anda:

```sql
SELECT *
FROM cron.job_run_details
ORDER BY start_time DESC
LIMIT 50;
```

## 7.6 pg_cron Guardrails

| Guardrail | Alasan |
|---|---|
| Job harus idempotent | Scheduler bisa retry/manual run |
| Set `lock_timeout` | Jangan menggantung blocking |
| Log lifecycle event | Auditability |
| Alert failed runs | Maintenance silent failure berbahaya |
| Jangan jalankan heavy archive di peak hours | I/O/lock risk |
| Pisahkan create future vs retention drop | Failure blast radius lebih kecil |

---

# 8. Option E - Kubernetes CronJob

## 8.1 Kapan Cocok

Gunakan Kubernetes CronJob jika:

```text
- aplikasi dan ops sudah Kubernetes-native
- tidak ingin scheduler di DB
- cloud provider berbeda-beda
- perlu secret management via K8s
- ingin observability via pod logs/metrics
- ingin menjalankan Java maintenance CLI atau psql job
```

## 8.2 Pattern: psql CronJob

```yaml
apiVersion: batch/v1
kind: CronJob
metadata:
  name: audit-event-partition-maintenance
spec:
  schedule: "5 0 * * *"
  concurrencyPolicy: Forbid
  successfulJobsHistoryLimit: 3
  failedJobsHistoryLimit: 5
  jobTemplate:
    spec:
      backoffLimit: 1
      template:
        spec:
          restartPolicy: Never
          containers:
            - name: partition-maintenance
              image: postgres:18
              env:
                - name: DATABASE_URL
                  valueFrom:
                    secretKeyRef:
                      name: app-db-secret
                      key: database-url
              command:
                - /bin/sh
                - -c
                - |
                  psql "$DATABASE_URL" \
                    -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 \
                    -c "SET lock_timeout = '5s'; SELECT app.ensure_audit_event_future_partitions(6);"
```

## 8.3 Pattern: Java Maintenance CLI

```text
java -jar app-maintenance.jar partition ensure-future \
  --table audit_event \
  --months-ahead 6
```

Kelebihan Java CLI:

| Kelebihan | Penjelasan |
|---|---|
| Bisa reuse config aplikasi | DB URL, tenant config, alerting |
| Bisa call domain/legal hold service | Retention lebih aman |
| Bisa emit metrics | Prometheus/OpenTelemetry |
| Bisa test dengan JUnit/Testcontainers | Lebih maintainable |
| Bisa punya dry-run mode | Aman untuk retention |

---

## 8.4 Kubernetes Guardrails

| Guardrail | Kenapa |
|---|---|
| `concurrencyPolicy: Forbid` | Hindari dua job maintenance overlap |
| `backoffLimit` kecil | Jangan retry agresif DDL |
| `activeDeadlineSeconds` | Hindari job menggantung |
| Separate service account | Least privilege |
| NetworkPolicy | Batasi akses DB |
| Secret rotation | Credential hygiene |
| Dry-run mode untuk retention | Cegah drop salah |
| Emit metrics/log structured | Observability |

---

# 9. AWS RDS / Aurora PostgreSQL

## 9.1 pg_partman + pg_cron

AWS documentation menyediakan panduan managing PostgreSQL partitions dengan `pg_partman` dan menyebut maintenance melalui `run_maintenance_proc`; `pg_cron` dapat menjadwalkan function/procedure tersebut untuk automatic partition creation, detach, atau remove old partitions. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/PostgreSQL_Partitions.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

High-level flow di AWS:

```text
1. Pastikan extension tersedia untuk engine version
2. Enable required extensions
3. Register parent table ke pg_partman
4. Configure premake/retention
5. Schedule run_maintenance_proc via pg_cron
6. Monitor cron job result
7. Monitor default partition/future horizon
```

## 9.2 AWS Practical Concerns

| Concern | Notes |
|---|---|
| Parameter group | pg_cron bisa butuh setting tertentu |
| Extension version | Berbeda antar engine version |
| Role privilege | RDS superuser terbatas |
| Maintenance window | DDL/index/retention harus dijadwalkan |
| Read replica lag | Backfill/retention bisa menaikkan WAL |
| PITR/backup | Drop partition harus sesuai recovery policy |
| CloudWatch | Export logs/metrics untuk alert |
| Blue/green | Test migration di clone sebelum prod |

## 9.3 RDS Guardrail Query

```sql
SELECT extname, extversion
FROM pg_extension
WHERE extname IN ('pg_partman', 'pg_cron', 'pg_stat_statements');
```

---

# 10. Azure Database for PostgreSQL

Azure documentation menjelaskan penggunaan pg_partman: `create_parent()` mengisi konfigurasi `part_config`/`part_config_sub`, dan `run_maintenance()` memeriksa parent table untuk membuat partitions baru atau menjalankan retention policy. ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/postgresql/configure-maintain/how-to-use-pg-partman?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Azure juga menyediakan daftar extension/module per engine version untuk Flexible Server; availability extension perlu dicek berdasarkan versi PostgreSQL yang dipakai. ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/postgresql/extensions/concepts-extensions-by-engine?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 10.1 Azure Practical Concerns

| Concern | Notes |
|---|---|
| Extension allowlist | Cek extension tersedia untuk engine version |
| Server parameter | Beberapa extension perlu preload/config |
| Managed identity/secret | Jika pakai external scheduler |
| Backup/PITR | Drop partition tetap bagian dari recovery policy |
| Maintenance window | Avoid heavy DDL at peak |
| Monitoring | Azure Monitor/log integration |
| Firewall/VNet | K8s/job access ke DB harus dibuka aman |

## 10.2 Azure Setup Check

```sql
SELECT name, default_version, installed_version
FROM pg_available_extensions
WHERE name IN ('pg_partman', 'pg_cron', 'pg_stat_statements');
```

---

# 11. On-prem PostgreSQL

## 11.1 Kelebihan On-prem

| Kelebihan | Penjelasan |
|---|---|
| Full extension control | Bisa compile/install pg_partman/pg_cron |
| OS scheduler available | cron/systemd timer |
| Backup/archive custom | Integrasi tape/NAS/object storage |
| Tablespace control | Hot/cold storage tier |
| Superuser control | Lebih fleksibel |

## 11.2 Risiko On-prem

| Risiko | Mitigasi |
|---|---|
| Extension version drift | Pin version, document upgrade |
| Manual ops | Automate via Ansible/Terraform |
| Backup not verified | Restore drill |
| Cron hidden on one host | Centralize scheduling/observability |
| Credential sprawl | Dedicated role/secrets manager |
| Lack of monitoring | Prometheus/exporter/logging |

## 11.3 OS Cron Example

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

psql "$DATABASE_URL" \
  -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 \
  -c "SET lock_timeout = '5s'; SELECT app.ensure_audit_event_future_partitions(6);"
```

Cron:

```cron
5 0 * * * /opt/app/bin/audit-event-partition-maintenance.sh >> /var/log/app/partition-maintenance.log 2>&1
```

# 12. GitHub/Maven Migration Pipeline

## 12.1 CI Flow

```text
Pull Request
│
├─ 1. Static SQL review
│  ├─ syntax
│  ├─ naming convention
│  ├─ partition bounds
│  └─ no dangerous DROP without approval
│
├─ 2. Ephemeral PostgreSQL
│  ├─ start container
│  ├─ apply migrations
│  ├─ insert sample data
│  └─ run smoke tests
│
├─ 3. Partition tests
│  ├─ current partition exists
│  ├─ future horizon exists
│  ├─ default partition behavior
│  ├─ insert routing
│  ├─ EXPLAIN pruning
│  └─ required indexes exist
│
├─ 4. App integration
│  ├─ MyBatis mapper tests
│  ├─ Testcontainers
│  ├─ SQLSTATE error mapping
│  └─ service validation from/to required
│
└─ 5. Deployment package
   ├─ migration scripts
   ├─ rollback notes
   ├─ runbook link
   └─ observability checks
```

## 12.2 Maven Testcontainers Test

High-level:

```java
@Test
void auditEventInsertRoutesToExpectedPartition() {
    UUID eventId = UUID.randomUUID();

    mapper.insertAuditEvent(new AuditEvent(
        eventId,
        tenantId,
        caseId,
        OffsetDateTime.parse("2026-10-15T10:00:00Z"),
        "SMOKE_TEST",
        "{}"
    ));

    String actualPartition = jdbcTemplate.queryForObject("""
        SELECT tableoid::regclass::text
        FROM app.audit_event
        WHERE event_id = ?
        """, String.class, eventId);

    assertEquals("app.audit_event_p2026_10", actualPartition);
}
```

## 12.3 EXPLAIN Test

```java
@Test
void searchByTenantAndTimePrunesPartitions() {
    String plan = String.join("\n", jdbcTemplate.queryForList("""
        EXPLAIN
        SELECT *
        FROM app.audit_event
        WHERE tenant_id = ?
          AND event_time >= ?
          AND event_time < ?
        """, String.class, tenantId, fromTime, toTime));

    assertTrue(plan.contains("audit_event_p2026_10"));
    assertFalse(plan.contains("audit_event_p2026_09"));
    assertFalse(plan.contains("audit_event_p2026_11"));
}
```

For more robust CI, use `EXPLAIN (FORMAT JSON)` and parse relation names.

---

# 13. Deployment Guardrails

## 13.1 Migration Session Safety

```sql
SET lock_timeout = '5s';
SET statement_timeout = '10min';
```

Use for DDL jobs so migration fails fast instead of blocking production indefinitely.

## 13.2 Non-transactional Migration Cases

These statements cannot run inside ordinary transaction blocks:

```text
- CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY
- DROP INDEX CONCURRENTLY
- some concurrent detach workflows
```

So migration tool must support non-transactional migrations.

## 13.3 Separate Heavy Operations

Do not combine:

```text
- create future partition
- build big indexes
- detach old partition
- archive/export
- drop table
```

in one giant migration. Separate them with observability and rollback.

---

# 14. Dedicated DB Roles

## 14.1 Application Role

```sql
CREATE ROLE app_service_role LOGIN;

GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA app TO app_service_role;
GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON app.audit_event TO app_service_role;
```

Do not grant broad DDL to application runtime unless needed.

## 14.2 Maintenance Role

```sql
CREATE ROLE partition_maintenance_role LOGIN;

GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA app TO partition_maintenance_role;
GRANT CREATE ON SCHEMA app TO partition_maintenance_role;

GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE
ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA app
TO partition_maintenance_role;
```

You may need additional privileges depending on exact operations. Use least privilege and test in staging.

## 14.3 Governance Rule

```text
Application role:
- normal DML
- no DROP/DETACH/CREATE INDEX on production tables

Maintenance role:
- partition DDL
- retention/archive
- lifecycle logging
- restricted to scheduler/job identity
```

---

# 15. Lifecycle Event Logging

Any automation should write lifecycle events.

```sql
INSERT INTO app.partition_lifecycle_event (
    table_name,
    partition_name,
    action,
    action_status,
    lower_bound,
    upper_bound,
    executed_by,
    details
)
VALUES (
    'audit_event',
    'audit_event_p2026_10',
    'CREATE',
    'SUCCESS',
    '2026-10-01 00:00:00+00',
    '2026-11-01 00:00:00+00',
    'k8s-cronjob:audit-event-partition-maintenance',
    '{"ticket":"AUTO","source":"ensure_future_partitions"}'::jsonb
);
```

This makes automation auditable.

---

# 16. Retention Automation with Legal Hold Guard

## 16.1 Legal Hold Table

```sql
CREATE TABLE app.partition_retention_hold (
    table_name      text        NOT NULL,
    partition_name  text        NOT NULL,
    hold_reason     text        NOT NULL,
    hold_by         text        NOT NULL,
    hold_until      timestamptz,
    created_at      timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    CONSTRAINT partition_retention_hold_pk
        PRIMARY KEY (table_name, partition_name)
);
```

## 16.2 Check Hold Before Detach/Drop

```sql
SELECT EXISTS (
    SELECT 1
    FROM app.partition_retention_hold
    WHERE table_name = 'audit_event'
      AND partition_name = 'audit_event_p2023_06'
      AND (hold_until IS NULL OR hold_until > now())
) AS has_active_hold;
```

## 16.3 Retention Job Flow

```text
1. Find partitions older than retention threshold
2. Exclude current/future/default partitions
3. Check legal hold
4. Dry-run output
5. Optional approval gate
6. DETACH partition
7. Export/archive
8. Verify checksum/manifest
9. DROP detached table only after verification
10. Log lifecycle event
```

For regulated data, automatic drop without legal hold/archive verification is too risky.

---

# 17. Archive Automation Pattern

## 17.1 Detach First

```sql
ALTER TABLE app.audit_event
DETACH PARTITION app.audit_event_p2023_06;
```

## 17.2 Export

```sql
COPY (
    SELECT *
    FROM app.audit_event_p2023_06
    ORDER BY event_time, event_id
) TO '/archive/audit_event_p2023_06.csv'
WITH (FORMAT csv, HEADER true);
```

In cloud/Kubernetes, export may be done via application job streaming to object storage rather than server-side file path, because managed PostgreSQL often cannot write to arbitrary filesystem paths.

## 17.3 Verify

```sql
SELECT
    COUNT(*) AS row_count,
    MIN(event_time) AS min_event_time,
    MAX(event_time) AS max_event_time,
    SUM(hashtext(event_id::text)) AS event_hash_sum
FROM app.audit_event_p2023_06;
```

Record manifest:

```json
{
  "table": "audit_event",
  "partition": "audit_event_p2023_06",
  "rowCount": 42398234,
  "minEventTime": "2023-06-01T00:00:00Z",
  "maxEventTime": "2023-06-30T23:59:59.999Z",
  "archiveLocation": "s3://company-archive/audit_event/2023/06/",
  "checksum": "..."
}
```

## 17.4 Drop After Verification

```sql
DROP TABLE app.audit_event_p2023_06;
```

---

# 18. Observability for Automation

## 18.1 Partition Health Query

```sql
WITH partitions AS (
    SELECT
        child.oid::regclass AS partition_name,
        pg_total_relation_size(child.oid) AS total_size_bytes,
        child.reltuples::bigint AS estimated_rows
    FROM pg_inherits i
    JOIN pg_class parent ON parent.oid = i.inhparent
    JOIN pg_class child ON child.oid = i.inhrelid
    WHERE parent.oid = 'app.audit_event'::regclass
)
SELECT
    partition_name,
    pg_size_pretty(total_size_bytes) AS total_size,
    estimated_rows
FROM partitions
ORDER BY partition_name::text;
```

## 18.2 Default Partition Alert

```sql
SELECT COUNT(*) AS default_rows
FROM app.audit_event_default;
```

## 18.3 Failed Lifecycle Events

```sql
SELECT *
FROM app.partition_lifecycle_event
WHERE action_status = 'FAILED'
ORDER BY executed_at DESC
LIMIT 50;
```

## 18.4 Cron/Job Failure

If using pg_cron:

```sql
SELECT *
FROM cron.job_run_details
ORDER BY start_time DESC
LIMIT 50;
```

If using Kubernetes:

```text
- Job status failed
- Pod exit code non-zero
- No successful run in last N hours
- Log contains ERROR
- Partition horizon below threshold
```

---

# 19. Platform Decision Guide

## 19.1 Recommended by environment

| Environment | Recommended automation |
|---|---|
| On-prem, DBA control strong | pg_partman + pg_cron or OS cron + PL/pgSQL |
| AWS RDS/Aurora | pg_partman + pg_cron if supported/enabled; otherwise K8s/external scheduler |
| Azure Flexible Server | pg_partman if extension available; external scheduler if DB scheduler constrained |
| Kubernetes-heavy platform | Kubernetes CronJob + SQL/Java maintenance CLI |
| Compliance-heavy org | Git-reviewed SQL + health alerts + manual approval for retention |
| High-volume daily partition | pg_partman or CronJob automation, not manual |
| Legal hold required | Custom retention job, not blind pg_partman drop |
| Multi-cloud/on-prem hybrid | Java maintenance service or portable K8s CronJob |

---

# 20. Automation Failure Modes

| Failure mode | Gejala | Pencegahan |
|---|---|---|
| Future partition job tidak jalan | Insert gagal/default rows naik | Horizon alert |
| Job overlap | DDL contention | `concurrencyPolicy: Forbid`, advisory lock |
| pg_partman config salah | Partition wrong interval | Review `part_config` |
| Retention drop terlalu agresif | Data hilang | Legal hold + dry-run + approval |
| Missing index on new partition | Query lambat bulan baru | Index template check |
| Scheduler silently failed | No future partitions | Job run alert |
| Permission denied | Maintenance errors | Dedicated role tested |
| Cloud extension unavailable | Deployment fails | Environment precheck |
| Long DDL blocks app | Latency spike | `lock_timeout`, maintenance window |
| Default partition grows | Future partition missing/late data | Default monitor + remediation job |
| Archive export incomplete | Compliance risk | Manifest/checksum validation |
| Pipeline only tests DDL | Query regression missed | EXPLAIN pruning CI test |

---

# 21. Advisory Lock for Maintenance Jobs

Untuk mencegah dua maintenance job berjalan bersamaan:

```sql
SELECT pg_try_advisory_lock(hashtext('audit_event_partition_maintenance')) AS acquired;
```

Dalam PL/pgSQL:

```sql
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION app.run_audit_event_partition_maintenance()
RETURNS void
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_lock_acquired boolean;
BEGIN
    SELECT pg_try_advisory_lock(hashtext('audit_event_partition_maintenance'))
    INTO v_lock_acquired;

    IF NOT v_lock_acquired THEN
        RAISE NOTICE 'Partition maintenance already running';
        RETURN;
    END IF;

    BEGIN
        PERFORM app.ensure_audit_event_future_partitions(6);

        PERFORM pg_advisory_unlock(hashtext('audit_event_partition_maintenance'));
    EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS THEN
        PERFORM pg_advisory_unlock(hashtext('audit_event_partition_maintenance'));
        RAISE;
    END;
END;
$$;
```

This is useful for pg_cron, Kubernetes CronJob, or manual DBA execution.

---

# 22. Java Maintenance CLI Pattern

## 22.1 Command

```bash
java -jar platform-maintenance.jar partition ensure \
  --schema app \
  --table audit_event \
  --months-ahead 6 \
  --dry-run false
```

## 22.2 Responsibilities

```text
1. Acquire advisory lock
2. Check current/future partition horizon
3. Generate missing partition DDL
4. Create required indexes
5. ANALYZE new partitions
6. Check default partition rows
7. Emit metrics
8. Write lifecycle events
9. Exit non-zero on critical failure
```

## 22.3 Pseudo-code

```java
public void ensureAuditEventPartitions(int monthsAhead, boolean dryRun) {
    if (!lockRepository.tryAcquire("audit_event_partition_maintenance")) {
        log.info("Maintenance already running");
        return;
    }

    try {
        List<PartitionPlan> plans = planner.planMonthlyPartitions(
            "app",
            "audit_event",
            OffsetDateTime.now(ZoneOffset.UTC),
            monthsAhead
        );

        for (PartitionPlan plan : plans) {
            if (dryRun) {
                log.info("Would create partition {}", plan.partitionName());
                continue;
            }

            partitionRepository.createPartitionIfMissing(plan);
            partitionRepository.createRequiredIndexes(plan);
            partitionRepository.analyzePartition(plan);
            lifecycleRepository.recordSuccess(plan);
        }

        long defaultRows = partitionRepository.countDefaultRows("app.audit_event_default");
        if (defaultRows > 0) {
            alertService.warn("Default partition contains rows: " + defaultRows);
        }
    } finally {
        lockRepository.release("audit_event_partition_maintenance");
    }
}
```

---

# 23. CI/CD Acceptance Tests for Partition Automation

| Test | Purpose |
|---|---|
| Create parent + partitions | DDL validity |
| Insert current month | Routing works |
| Insert future month | Future horizon works |
| Insert out-of-bound | Default/failure behavior expected |
| Query by date range | Pruning works |
| Check required index | New partition indexed |
| Run automation twice | Idempotency |
| Simulate job overlap | Advisory lock works |
| Simulate legal hold | Retention skips partition |
| Simulate old partition | Retention candidate identified |
| Validate rollback | Emergency path clear |

---

# 24. Production Runbook: Future Partition Missing

```text
Symptoms:
- insert error: no partition found
- default partition row count increasing
- horizon alert critical

Immediate actions:
1. Identify missing date range.
2. Create missing partition.
3. Create required indexes.
4. ANALYZE new partition.
5. If data entered default, move rows out.
6. Verify insert routing.
7. Run EXPLAIN pruning.
8. Fix automation scheduler.
9. Record lifecycle/incident event.
```

SQL:

```sql
SELECT
    min(event_time),
    max(event_time),
    count(*)
FROM app.audit_event_default;
```

Create missing partition:

```sql
SELECT app.ensure_audit_event_month_partition('2026-10-01 00:00:00+00');
```

---

# 25. Production Runbook: pg_partman Job Failed

```text
Symptoms:
- cron job failure
- no future partitions created
- part_config exists but horizon low

Steps:
1. Check extension installed/version.
2. Check part_config for parent table.
3. Manually run maintenance in staging first.
4. Run maintenance manually in prod if safe.
5. Check errors in DB logs / cron job details.
6. Verify new partitions and indexes.
7. Fix scheduler/permission/config.
8. Add alert if absent.
```

SQL:

```sql
SELECT extname, extversion
FROM pg_extension
WHERE extname = 'pg_partman';

SELECT *
FROM partman.part_config
WHERE parent_table = 'app.audit_event';

CALL partman.run_maintenance_proc();
```

---

# 26. Production Runbook: Retention Automation Wants to Drop Partition

```text
Before allowing drop:
1. Is partition older than retention threshold?
2. Is it current/future/default? If yes, stop.
3. Is there legal hold? If yes, skip.
4. Is archive required? If yes, verify archive manifest.
5. Are row count/checksum recorded?
6. Is approval/ticket present?
7. Is rollback impossible after drop acknowledged?
8. Is backup/PITR coverage understood?
```

Safer default for regulatory systems:

```text
DETACH → ARCHIVE → VERIFY → WAIT → DROP
```

not:

```text
DROP immediately
```

---

# 27. Recommended Automation Architecture for Your Stack

Given stack: Java 17+, Maven, GitHub, Jersey, MyBatis, Camunda, PostgreSQL, Linux, Docker, Kubernetes, on-prem/AWS/Azure.

Recommended approach:

```text
1. Use GitHub-reviewed SQL for parent table and initial partition design.
2. Use Kubernetes CronJob or Java maintenance CLI for future partition creation.
3. Use pg_partman only if extension availability is consistent across environments.
4. Keep retention/drop custom because regulatory/legal hold usually needs domain checks.
5. Store lifecycle events in DB.
6. Expose internal Jersey endpoint for partition health.
7. Add Maven/Testcontainers tests for insert routing and pruning.
8. Alert on future horizon/default partition/failed lifecycle jobs.
```

Why not blindly use pg_partman for everything?

```text
pg_partman is excellent for mechanical partition maintenance.
But regulated retention often needs:
- legal hold
- archive manifest
- approval
- audit trail
- domain-specific skip rules
```

So a hybrid pattern is often safest:

```text
pg_partman/custom job for future partition creation
custom domain-aware job for retention/archive/drop
```

---

# 28. Final Checklist Seri 13

| Checklist | Status |
|---|---|
| Automation strategy selected |
| Extension availability checked per env |
| Future partition creation is idempotent |
| Required indexes created on new partitions |
| ANALYZE runs after partition creation/load |
| Default partition monitored |
| Future horizon alert exists |
| Scheduler failure alert exists |
| Advisory lock prevents overlapping jobs |
| Lifecycle events are recorded |
| Retention has legal hold guard |
| Archive has manifest/checksum |
| DDL migrations use lock timeout |
| Concurrent index operations handled outside transaction |
| CI tests insert routing |
| CI tests EXPLAIN pruning |
| Kubernetes/AWS/Azure/on-prem constraints documented |
| Rollback/runbook exists |

---

# Kesimpulan Seri 13

Automation partitioning harus diperlakukan sebagai **platform capability**, bukan script sampingan.

Rule paling penting:

```text
1. Future partition creation must be automatic or strictly Git-reviewed.
2. Every automation must be idempotent.
3. Default partition must be monitored.
4. New partition must get required indexes.
5. ANALYZE must run after create/load/attach.
6. Retention automation must be legal-hold-aware.
7. Do not blindly auto-drop regulated data.
8. Use scheduler appropriate to environment: pg_cron, Kubernetes CronJob, CI/CD, or external scheduler.
9. pg_partman is strong for mechanical partition maintenance, but domain retention may still need custom logic.
10. CI/CD must test insert routing and partition pruning, not only DDL syntax.
```
